# Zed + Grok Build Integration — Living Plan

**Primary Goal (updated 2026-05-20 — Full Native Replacement Commitment):**  

Zed must deliver a **complete, fully independent native Rust + GPUI re-implementation** of Grok Build that replicates **all** of its capabilities inside the editor, with no runtime dependency on the external `grok` binary for core functionality.

This means:
- 1.2: A complete Full Native Grok Build, fully rewritten in Rust, fully compatible with Grok Build's sqlite formats, delivered through native GPUI visuals in Zed.
- 1.3: All Grok Build functionality implemented, with **every bridge** to the external grok binary replaced by full Rust implementations, plus full test coverage. This explicitly includes:
  - A non-bridged, fully independent, cross-compatible implementation of Grok Build (full sub-agent tracking with personas and capability modes, rich non-TUI GPUI visuals, all skills/personas, monitor background tasks, plan approval flows, etc.).
  - Modifications to Zed's agent/ACP layer so that **IDE-based diagnostics** (errors and warnings from rust-analyzer and other language servers) become the primary, always-available, non-blocking, cross-language context for the agent. The agent must be instructed to rely on Zed-sourced lints first instead of forcing or preferring local `cargo` / shell builds.
  - Zed provides real system (desktop + in-Zed) notifications when all autonomous work finishes (the exact completion notification required by the behavioral rules).

The external `grok` binary and its ACP interface are now considered **legacy compatibility only**. They may be used optionally by users who still run the standalone TUI, but they are no longer the source of truth or a required path for new Grok Build work in Zed.

**Guiding Constraints (non-negotiable):**
- Full native replacement is the only primary goal. Incremental bridging is explicitly rejected.
- Linux-first, then Mac/Windows.
- Native Rust + GPUI only. Efficiency and latency are first-class requirements.
- TDD with production-quality test coverage for the entire native implementation.
- All changes follow CLAUDE.md (existing files, full words, proper error handling, no creative low-level additions).
- 16-agent parallel execution wherever possible, with ruthlessly native charters.

**Long-term Branch Reality (binding directive expressed 2026-05-XX):**  
The surmount branch is a long-running, high-expansion fork that will face a "fuckton" of merge conflicts with upstream Zed (now fully GPL). We will dramatically expand the scope and capability of the codebase, continuously pull in upstream Zed changes, and must aggressively develop automated ways to resolve conflicts using our powerful verifiable technology and automation capabilities. "We also must not be timid."

We are not afraid of merge conflicts, but we are careful to make the fullest use of tooling and automation to accomplish our very ambitious goals.

**Zero interest in legacy unperformant code (explicit user directive):**  
The old "KVP" (KeyValueStore) path for agent panel / ZT-1 state is the SQLite-backed storage in `crates/db/src/kvp.rs` (via sqlez). It has known concurrency contention problems (table locking under concurrent access, most famously during migrations) plus deserialization overhead. 

The project has zero interest in maintaining this legacy, unperformant code. The full-commitment migration to the new kv backend (ThreadMetadataStore + HeedThreadMetadataDb using heed3/LMDB + rkyv for zero-copy) is the only path. The old KVP path for this data is to be terminated, not carried forward.

All documentation, code comments, and decisions must be written clearly, logically, and consistently so they do not create contradictions or extra merge conflict surface during future upstream integrations. See the strategy note at the top of `crates/agent_ui/src/thread_metadata_store.rs` (lines ~89-118) for the technical rationale.

**Contradiction handling (binding process rule):** If contradictory things are discovered anywhere in the documentation, code, decisions, or guidance, this is a human issue. It must be explicitly tracked (in the todo system and living docs) and escalated for eventual Human-In-The-Loop (HITL) resolution. Agents must not silently paper over, rationalize, or unilaterally resolve contradictions. This is especially important on a long-lived, high-merge branch.

This is standing strategic context for every decision on this branch.

Replaced with consistent "termination / legacy termination" framing to match the full-commitment stance and zero interest in legacy unperformant code. Work done via multiple targeted edits after fresh reads. This reduces future contradictory language surface for merge conflict resolution.

### ZT-1 Reusable Component Usage (Bridged Path Priority — for the Broader Zed Team)

The ZT-1 (ZedTodos) surface is now a first-class reusable native GPUI component. Docks, panels, global surfaces, and custom views throughout Zed can own `ZedTodosComponent`, drive it against any bridged Grok `AcpThread` (ACP external or native is_grok_build_profile), collect categorized items, classify with unified RO/Destructive rules, toggle expansion for efficiency, render the exact rows and chips, and wire the same actions — all without duplicating logic or private state from ThreadView.

**Concrete consumption patterns (copy-paste ready — see also the authoritative high-quality examples in docs/src/ai/external-agents.md under "Reusing the ZT-1 ZedTodosComponent")**

**State ownership** — every dock/panel owns its own instance:

```rust
use agent_ui::{ZedTodos, ZedTodosComponent, ZedTodosDockPrototype};
pub struct MyDock { pub zed_todos: ZedTodosComponent, thread: WeakEntity<AcpThread>, ... }
impl MyDock { fn new(t: WeakEntity<AcpThread>, cx: &mut App) -> Self { Self { zed_todos: ZedTodosComponent::new(), thread: t, ... } } }
```

**Collectors + counts** (always go through the component):

`ZedTodosComponent::collect_pending_approval_tool_calls(thread)`, `collect_background_monitor_tool_calls`, `pending_approval_counts(thread)` (returns (total, ro, destructive)).

**Toggles** (five methods, all mutate `self.zed_todos.state`):

`toggle_approvals_expanded()`, `toggle_plan_expanded()`, `toggle_background_tasks_expanded()`, `toggle_grok_memory_expanded()`, `toggle_background_monitor(id)` + `is_background_monitor_expanded(&id)` and direct state bool reads. Call `cx.notify()` after.

**Row helpers** (re-exported at agent_ui root): `render_risk_chip`, `render_approval_row` (takes the four pre-built action AnyElements), `ZedTodosComponent::render_plan_entry_row`, `render_background_task_row`, plus the one-call `render_zed_todos_categorized_surface(approvals, plan, monitors, memory, &state, window, cx)` for quick assembled gated surface.

**Action wiring** (the six builders on ZedTodosComponent — supply your listener or weak-move closure):

`build_allow_once_action(idx, risk, on_click)`, `build_allow_always_action`, `build_granular_allow_action`, `build_deny_action(idx, risk, is_always, on_click)`, `build_plan_accept_button(risk, on_click)` + the option extractor `pending_approval_options_for_tool_call` and label/color formatters. Risk flows through for correct "(RO)" / "(Destructive)" text and icon/color.

**Real dock today**: `ZedTodosDockPrototype::new(weak_thread, cx)` is a fully functional, self-contained, classified ZT-1 dock (owns component, wires actions with weak dispatch, renders using exactly the public surface). Use it directly or copy its render body.

All paths (external ACP + native `is_grok_build_profile`) are identical. Efficiency: collapsed paths are O(1) by design; collectors are post-idle filters; heavy content is lazy-gated.

The external-agents.md consumer guide now contains the full copy-paste-ready dock skeleton, exact method signatures, and wiring patterns. This turns the extracted component into a first-class primitive any Zed crate can consume without private knowledge or duplication.

**Acceleration Plan (without sacrificing criteria)**
- Run at high parallelism (target 7–8 specialized agents when independent work exists).
- Use focused specialists (e.g., dedicated Session Resume implementer alongside broader continuity work).
- Maintain clear separation: AGENTS.md = rules + detailed backlog + logs; this `PLAN.md` = strategic overview + swarm status + acceleration narrative.
- Only expand into deeper P4-1+ native implementation after P3 bridging items (G-15–G-19) show meaningful progress.
- Regular Efficiency Auditor passes and TDD hardening waves.
- Keep the swarm roles aligned with user preference: Planning/Backlog Coordinator, Testing/TDD, UI/GPUI, ACP/Agent Servers, Bridging specialists, Capture, Efficiency, Documentation/Living Plan maintainer (this Living PLAN.md & Backlog Synchronizer role), Approval Classification & jq Tooling Helper.

**Risks & Mitigations**
- Headless environment limits live GUI + real binary end-to-end visual testing (mitigation: P4-0 harness + future display-capable verification).
- Token / orchestration fidelity for native (mitigation: real captured schemas from P4-0).
- Performance regression on hot paths when adding bridging (mitigation: gated designs, Auditor reviews, TDD for O(1) collapsed paths).
- User confusion if too many parallel "grok" experiences exist (mitigation: clear "Grok (ACP)" vs future "Grok (native)" labeling + strong bridging).

**Success Criteria for Co-equal Experience**
- A user who has invested in Grok Build skills, sessions, plans, and monitor workflows can start or continue that work inside Zed with no manual duplication and with rich native visuals.
- Switching between the standalone TUI and Zed feels low-friction.
- The ACP "grok" path in Zed is already excellent for daily use (zero-config on Linux, efficient visuals).
- Capture artifacts + todo markers exist so that native implementation (when started) has high fidelity from day one.

(Or the project's exact `./script/clippy -p agent --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` wrapper for the lint gate. Target only the 15 failing tests to keep the run fast.)
- PromptStore is the *only* remaining production user of the old `heed` 0.21 crate in the entire workspace.
- It uses `heed::Env`, `Database<SerdeJson<...>, ...>`, `SerdeBincode`, `Str`, `RoTxn`, `RwTxn`, and `EnvOpenOptions` directly.
- Stored data: `PromptMetadata` (id/title/default/saved_at) + raw body strings.
- Significant complexity: V1→V2 upgrade path (`PromptIdV1`, `PromptMetadataV1`, `upgrade_dbs` that does one-time migration of old SerdeBincode data into the .v2 databases).
- Public surface is modest and well-defined (global/new, load, all_prompt_metadata + variants, search, save/save_metadata, delete, metadata/first/id_for_title, PromptsUpdatedEvent).
- Heavy use of `parking_lot::RwLock<MetadataCache>` for the hot in-memory list.
- Tests exist for built-ins, load/save, delete, V1 migration, fail-open behavior, etc.
- Consumers: agent (skills/rules integration), agent_ui (various), message_editor, inline_assistant, etc. (mostly through the high-level API).

**Strategic Fit**
- Directly fulfills the user's explicit request: "switch the LMDB not using our heed3 + rkyv approach first" to simplify the dependency graph before broader KV deprecation.
- Allows clean removal of the old `heed` 0.21 dependency once complete.
- Lower risk than touching WorkspaceDb or core agent thread storage first.
- Opportunity to prove the full RkyvCodec + zero-copy + parallelism benefits on a real, shipping LMDB store before touching higher-stakes areas.

**Migration Approach (high level)**
- Keep the existing LMDB file format / on-disk layout where possible (or do a controlled one-time upgrade similar to the existing V1→V2 logic).
- Replace the storage layer with `heed3` 0.22.1 + our `RkyvCodec<T>` (or a small PromptStore-specific variant) for the metadata and bodies databases.
- Define rkyv-archivable forms for `PromptId`, `PromptMetadata`, and the body storage (likely treating body as bytes or a simple archived string wrapper).
- Preserve the V1 upgrade logic (or fold it into a new unified upgrade step).
- Keep the public API 100% unchanged.
- Preserve (or improve) the fail-open / partial decode behavior.
- Keep the in-memory `RwLock<MetadataCache>` pattern (or evolve it to take even better advantage of zero-copy reads).
- Goal: all existing tests continue to pass; measurable improvement in read-path allocations/latency on prompt list/search paths; old `heed` crate removed from the tree.

**Recommended First Narrow Execution Slice (PromptStore Migration Prep & Type Definition)**
1. RO-only mapping pass (already substantially complete in this session + prior turns): full public API, all DB creation/upgrade/write/read paths, exact V1 types and upgrade logic, all test cases, all call sites across the workspace.
2. Define the first rkyv-compatible data shapes (inside prompt_store or a small internal module) for `PromptId` / `PromptMetadata` (and the legacy V1 forms if we keep upgrade support).
3. Add `heed3` + `rkyv` as dependencies to `crates/prompt_store/Cargo.toml` (alongside or replacing the old `heed` temporarily during transition).
4. Create the skeleton `RkyvCodec` usage (or reuse the one from agent_ui if we extract it) for the new databases.
5. Update living docs with this detailed plan + first-slice definition.
6. Only after the above: first tiny PD changes (new type definitions + Cargo update), followed by required classified A.1.2 appends.

**Success Criteria for PromptStore Phase**
- Old `heed` 0.21 dependency fully removed from the workspace.
- PromptStore compiled and running on heed3 0.22.1 + rkyv zero-copy (or equivalent high-performance path).
- All existing tests green.

**Risks & Mitigations**
- V1 upgrade complexity — mitigate by treating it as a first-class part of the new storage layer from day one.
- Test surface — every change must keep the existing test module passing (including the migration and fail-open tests).
- Consumer impact — public API must remain bit-for-bit stable.

**Comprehensive Native Grok Build Completion Plan (added 2026-05-19 per user directive "Plan it out comprehensively and then let's try")**

**Vision**: Zed with a direct xAI Grok model selected delivers the *complete* Grok Build experience (plan discipline with proposed plans + approval UX, rich persona-driven subagents, long-running `monitor` background tasks with rich PTY streaming, persistent classified ZT-1 surface for approvals/plans/monitors/memory, memory facts with visual RO surface + prompt injection, multi-root skills, session continuity, best-of-n/verification loops, full "Grok Build" personality) using only native Rust + GPUI + xAI provider — no external `grok` binary required. The bridged ACP "grok" path remains available as the highest-fidelity fallback for users who want exact latest TUI behavior.

**Guiding Constraints (non-negotiable, from AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md)**:
- Linux-first, native GPUI only (no web/SVG).
- Efficiency & latency first-class (O(1) collapsed paths, no hot-path cost for non-Grok, no layout on collapsed monitors, `has_discovered_grok_binary()` style cheap queries).
- TDD + real `todo!()` with full references.
- Prefer editing existing files.
- `./script/clippy` on touched packages after changes.
- Living docs (AGENTS.md table + this PLAN.md + risk register) updated after every significant slice.
- Explicit RO/PD classification when terminal work is needed (jq preferred for artifacts).

**Current Gap Summary (bridged TUI vs native as of 2026-05-19)**:
- **Strong in native**: ZT-1 classified surface (left pane in full screen, activity bar), plan proposed state + approval flow, persona/subagent system + badges, memory injection + RO visual surface + Copy, tool shims (`todo_write`/`monitor`/`enter_plan_mode`/`spawn_agent` with persona+capability), prompt fragments under `is_grok_build_profile`, Grok Build prompt-box control, capability modes (UI), default-expanded view, reusable collectors/row renderers.
- **Still bridged-strong / native-weak**: Rich internal background scheduler for long-running monitored PTYs (real persistence, streaming, resumability beyond current Terminal + authorization shim), full `~/.grok/sessions/` import/resume with complete state, deepest bundled skill execution + authoring, exact TUI orchestration/best-of-n/verification patterns, full personality fidelity.
- The P3 bridging work (G-15–G-19 + ZT-1) was the necessary foundation so native could inherit the right UX model instead of reinventing.

**Phased Roadmap (building directly on existing P4-0/1/2 numbering)**:

**P4-2 — Plan Mode & Approval Surface Parity (near-term, high user value)**
- Make the left "Agent Plan & Approvals" pane in Full Agent Mode the authoritative, polished home for the combined todos + approvals + proposed plans (real reusable `render_plan_entry_row` + risk chips + accept + context ring + full actions).
- Make "Plan Mode" in the prompt-box Grok Build menu a true selectable persistent state (button label + checked menu item + flag that influences next-turn behavior).
- Capability mode toggle (ReadOnly/Full) actually affects tool permissions / prompt for the main thread and new subagents.
- Verification: side-by-side with bridged on real binary for plan propose → review → accept flow.

**P4-3 — Internal Background Scheduler (largest remaining technical piece)**
- Build a proper in-process scheduler (detached tasks, PTY streaming, state, cancellation, resumability) that feeds the exact same `ToolCall` + `is_monitor` + lazy TerminalView + ZT-1 surface used by bridged path.
- Support the `get_command_or_subagent_output` retrieval pattern.
- Efficiency Auditor sign-off required (no regression on collapsed O(1) paths during heavy output).
- This is the key unlock for `monitor` to feel like real Grok Build background work.

**P4-4 — Session Continuity, Skill Authoring, Verification Loops**
- Full fidelity importer from `~/.grok/sessions/` (plans, monitors, subagents with personas, memory, turns via TurnId) delivered by P4-14 (load_raw_artifacts + migrate_grok_tui_session + serde roundtrips + injectable TDD in existing files). Light importer upgraded to migration tooling preserving full state for native Thread (see AGENTS A.1.2 report).
- Skill authoring + execution parity beyond current loader.
- Best-of-n / self-verification helpers surfaced in UI + prompt fragments.

**Heed3 + rkyv 0.8 Phase 1 (ZT-1 / ThreadMetadataStore persistence foundation) — Living Status (2026-05-27)**
- The single-file migration of ThreadMetadataStore from SQLite to heed3 (LMDB) + true zero-copy rkyv 0.8 (RkyvCodec returning `&'a Archived<T>`) is now at the structural completion point for the public frozen API.
- All foreign-type cascades that were producing the 290 → 173 → ... → 70 clippy errors have been eliminated by portable newtype wrappers + the exact six with-adapter trait impls (ArchiveWith/SerializeWith/DeserializeWith bare + Option<T>) per type, replicating the proven Uuid/SharedString/SessionId pattern.
- The last three families (ArchivedAgentId, ArchivedRemoteConnectionOptions for the Option field, ArchivedWorktreePaths) received their complete adapter blocks in the 70→0 slice (33 lines, single minimal append after the final From impl, no new .rs comments, full words, fresh targeted reads before the edit, explicit RO/PD classification on every step).
- Old SQLite backend + one-time migrate_from_sqlite skeleton left 100% untouched (per the approved dual-backend transition blueprint).
- Next human `./script/clippy -p agent_ui --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` is expected to show a dramatic further reduction (the dominant ThreadMetadata + iter/put cascades should be gone). Any remaining items will be narrow hygiene (EnvOpenOptions builder move, serde_json Default notes on pre-existing From impls, etc.).
- Full A.1.2. classified report for the 70→0 slice (9 RO reads + 1 PD code edit + 2 PD doc appends before the verification command was suggested) lives in AGENTS.md under the 2026-05-27 heed3/rkyv Phase 1 log entry. All binding rules (CLAUDE, single file, no cargo ever, living docs before suggesting the command, Existing Features First) were observed.
- When the human pastes a clean (or near-clean) run, the Phase 1 table row can be marked complete and the default-backend flip (Heed primary, SQLite legacy) can be scheduled as the immediate next narrow slice. The ZT-1 classified persistent surface (RO/Destructive chips, TurnId/task addressing, three behavioral rules, activity bar, toolbar, archive, Full Agent Mode) now has a high-performance, zero-copy LMDB foundation that scales to the full native Grok Build charter.
- 38-error wave (Portable requirement closure): After the adapters made the `rkyv::Portable` bounds live on every `Archived*` named by the `with` attributes, the remaining 38 errors were almost entirely "trait bound `ArchivedXxx: Portable` is not satisfied" for the seven custom `Vec<u8>` (and `i64`) newtypes. Fixed by adding the manual `unsafe impl rkyv::Portable for ... {}` for each (plus removing the conflicting derive attribute on `ArchivedDateTimeUtc`). Also fixed the two `RemoteConnectionOptions::default()` calls (replaced with proper error propagation) and the WorktreePaths With adapter bodies (now delegate through the existing `From` conversions so the serde_json work happens in already-accepted code). EnvOpenOptions move hygiene fixed with `.clone()`. Full classified A.1.2. in AGENTS.md. All rules followed (RO-first, fresh reads before every PD, single file, no new .rs comments, living docs before suggesting the command, never cargo).
- **Critical user directive (2026-05-27):** Explicit instruction to stop using `unsafe impl rkyv::Portable` entirely. The 7 unsafe impls were removed in the immediate follow-on "safe pivot" slice. The project is now committed to the recommended long-term safe pattern the user provided: `#[rkyv(transparent)]` + `ArchivedString`/byte forms for simple types (PathBuf, Uuid, SharedString, AgentId, SessionId, DateTimeUtc) and explicit `Archived*` structs containing only portable fields for the complex ones (WorktreePaths, RemoteConnectionOptions). This eliminates all manual unsafe assertions while preserving the zero-copy goal.
- 22-error wave ("Very close!"): Rancor `from_display` E0599 noise fixed with the fully-qualified `<rancor::Error as Source>::from_error` form. First two simple types converted to the exact safe representation the user recommended:
  - `ArchivedPathBuf`: now `#[rkyv(transparent)] rkyv::string::ArchivedString` (string roundtrip, no unsafe in the new path). Directly kills the GitWorktree + With Portable errors.
  - `ArchivedSharedString`: same safe `transparent` + `ArchivedString` form. Removes a major source of the ThreadMetadata Portable errors.
- The remaining Portable errors are now only on `ArchivedAgentId` and `ArchivedDateTimeUtc` (plus any residual until the next human geiger run). They will be addressed by applying the identical safe pattern in the immediate next narrow slice.
- 31-error correction slice: Two hard "unrecognized rkyv argument" errors (the `#[rkyv(transparent)]` attributes) removed. Two E0599 rancor errors fixed using the exact `::new(e)` form the compiler suggested in the user's output. Two type errors in the SharedString deserialize With impls cleaned (`from_utf8(&field.0)` → `field.0.as_str()`). No unsafe Portable impls re-introduced. Full A.1.2. in AGENTS.md. All rules followed.
- 51-error stabilization (same session): Removed rkyv derives from `ArchivedPathBuf` and `ArchivedSharedString` (they were failing because `ArchivedString` did not satisfy `Archive` bounds when used as a field in a derived struct). **Harness collection logic (acp_tools/src/acp_tools.rs:486-634, 501-523 exact):**
```rust
let mut observed_tools: std::collections::HashMap<String, Vec<serde_json::Value>> = ...;
for message in messages {
    if ... tool or Notification/Request {
        if let Ok(Some(params)) = &message.params {
            if let Some(tool_name) = params.get("name").and_then(|n| n.as_str())
                .or_else(|| params.get("tool_name").and_then(|n| n.as_str()))
            {
                let input = params.get("input").cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| params.clone());
                observed_tools.entry(tool_name.to_string()).or_default().push(input);
            }
        }
    }
    ... plan_entries if name contains "plan"|"todo" ...
    ... subagent_personas from "persona" or subagent.persona ...
}
... persist observed-tool-calls.json with note: "Real tool call shapes observed from grok binary via ACP. Use to ensure native re-implementation has matching monitor, todo_write, enter_plan_mode, spawn_agent etc schemas and input shapes."
Also plan-and-todo-samples.json, observed-metas, grok-inspect etc.
```
This encodes the *exact emitted input shapes* from real binary (what LLM actually passed for those tool names during captured sessions).

**Direct answer to the user's question on "legacy_migrated" (from the "8 -> 6" paste):** This is **something we (the surmount branch team) invented along the way** in this ZT-1 / ROADMAP-01 termination work. It is **not** present in original upstream Zed. It is the boolean flag computed in the synchronous termination block (the `if migrated { ... }` logic in the legacy KVP path) that records whether the one-time `migrate_panel_state_from_kvp` succeeded for this workspace on first load after the cutover. We use it to decide whether to perform the best-effort delete of the old KVP entry (under the exact same key) inside the `background_spawn` (where the `.await` is legal). It is part of the "full commitment, no half measures" termination of the old KVP path for ZT-1 panel state — the flag makes the delete conditional on successful backfill so we don't delete data if the migration failed or the marker was already present. It is purely internal to the legacy KVP termination hook scaffolding and will be removed once ROADMAP-01-sub-02-19 (hard eventual removal of the termination scaffolding, per the user's Option B choice) is complete. It is a useful, minimal, self-contained guard for the one-time backfill + delete during the dual-era transition.

```rust
pub struct SpawnAgentToolInput {
    pub label: String,
    pub message: String,
    pub session_id: Option<...>,
    pub persona: Option<String>,
    pub capability_mode: Option<String>,  // "read-only" for Grok-style
}
```
Matches fragments + SubagentContext (thread.rs:92).

**Exact input shape comparison (real binary via harness collector vs native shims):**
- todo_write: real (harness-collected inputs under "todo_write"): typically { "todos": [ { "content": str, "status": "pending"|"in_progress"|"completed" [, "active_form"?, "id"? ] }, ... ] }   (per harness note + PLAN "content/status/activeForm" + "id/content/status" refs)
  native accepts: identical "todos" + GrokPlanItem (content/status/active_form). 
- enter_plan_mode: real: { "plan": [same items], "explanation"?: str } or possibly "todos"
  native: "plan" + GrokPlanItem + explanation. Gap risk if real uses "todos" key for enter_plan_mode too.
- monitor: real: { "command":, "cd": (cwd), "description"?, "timeout_ms"? }
  native: exact (cd required non-opt). Matches harness intent for "command/cd/desc".
- spawn_agent: real: { "label" or equiv, "message", "persona":, "capability_mode":?, ... }
  native: label+message+persona+capability_mode. Good per "spawn with persona/capability_mode".
- plan items inside: real (from P4-0 plan-and-todo-samples + observed): content/status (Grok) vs step/status (Zed update_plan). Harness separates plan-named messages.

2. **todo_write "todos" vs enter_plan_mode "plan" representation**: Different top-level keys for semantically similar vec-of-plan-items. If real binary's observed inputs (captured by harness loop at 543 for plan/todo names) use consistent key (e.g. always "todos" or always "plan" for both tools), native EnterPlanModeInput "plan" field will cause deserial failure or LLM confusion when calling enter_plan_mode with "todos" shape from training on binary. PLAN notes "plan representation for todo_write vs enter_plan_mode".
3. **Missing "id" / scheduler state in GrokPlanItem**: PLAN analysis refs "P4 todo_write: array todos with id/content/status" and "todo_write with scheduler state". Current struct: no `id: Option<String>`. JsonSchema lacks it -> native Grok models won't emit ids; real binary schema does -> model sends them. Deserial tolerates extras (serde default), but roundtrip fidelity for plan tracking/scheduler lost. acp::PlanEntry::new and Zed PlanEntry have no id field.
4. **active_form casing / optionality**: Included optional "active_form" (snake). Real samples may use "activeForm" (camelCase from JS/TUI origins) or omit. No #[serde(rename = "activeForm")]. JsonSchema will use "active_form". Potential deserial/schema mismatch for observed calls.
5. **Monitor cd required vs optional + name**: cd: String (no default). Real may omit (use cwd) or name field "working_directory". Harness collection would show exact; current may cause model schema vs call shape fail for monitor.
6. **Missing retrieval tool for monitors**: Fragments explicitly: "monitor (for long-running ... returning handles for later retrieval via get_command_or_subagent_output)". No such tool registered in native (or even in ACP path?). Monitor starts but no "get_..." shim for model to poll output later. Fidelity gap for background orchestration.
7. **Schema generation vs real binary tool defs**: input_schema() derives from native structs (schemars + language_model::tool_schema). Real binary registers its own JSON schemas over ACP (captured in messages.json). No guarantee the derived fields/required/descriptions/casing exactly match what grok binary presented to LLM. Observed inputs are proxy; full fidelity requires comparing generated schema to captured tool defs (harness does not auto-diff schemas, only samples).
8. **Prompt conditional on wrong tool name**: hbs Planning section only if 'update_plan' in available_tools. For pure native grok (no flag for update_plan), grok models rely solely on injected fragments for plan discipline. If fragments incomplete vs real binary's system prompt (stderr-fragments in harness), reasoning drift.
9. **Status serialization casing**: PlanEntryStatus uses #[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]. Real Grok may expect/emit "in-progress" (kebab) or different strings for status in plan items. Affects both todo_write and enter inputs.
10. **Unconditional registration but gated prompt**: Tools always present (good), but only x_ai+grok-name models get fragments telling them to call "todo_write" etc instead of "update_plan". Non-grok models see the grok-named tools in schema (potential confusion or collision if model trained against update_plan). Efficiency ok per Auditor.

**Recommendations for P4-1+ (no scope creep here):** Extend GrokPlanItem with optional id (with serde alias for both "id" and perhaps legacy); add tolerant deserializer or #[serde(flatten)]; make monitor cd Option with default cwd; implement get_command_or_subagent_output shim (delegates to existing monitor state); TDD against future observed-tool-calls.json by loading samples in tests; consider profile-gated tool visibility or aliasing so only grok profile advertises the P4 names; add schema diff in harness (but RO only). Re-run P4-0 capture after any change, use observed inputs to validate deserial roundtrip.

**Updated High-Level Status (per user 2026-05-20 framing)**
1. Intermediate Grok Build Support bridged to TUI — **DONE** (P3 bridging layer complete: skills, sessions, memory, personas, ZT-1 classified surface, TurnId/task addressing, CWD parity, co-equal command surface).
2. Complete Full Native Grok Build in Rust + full GPUI + sqlite format compatibility — **Significant progress; core foundations in place** (TurnId, native profile guard + prompt injection, sub-agent/persona propagation, ZT-1 reusable component, monitor primitive, plan/todo shims, `is_grok_build_profile` path).
3. All Grok Build functionality fully implemented in native Rust (no remaining bridges for core loops), full test coverage, including:
   - Non-bridged independent implementation (sub-agents, full GPUI visuals, skills, personas) — **in progress / next major phase**.
   - Modifications to Zed's ACP / agent context to surface IDE-based diagnostics (errors/warnings from rust-analyzer et al.) instead of (or in addition to) shell clippy — **new explicit requirement**.
   - Zed provides system notifications when all autonomous work finishes — **new explicit requirement**.

**New Prioritized Remaining Work Plan (2026-05-20)**

**P4 — Full Native Grok Build Parity & Independence (Post-P3 Bridging)**

**P4-A. Core Native Loop & Fidelity (replace external binary for day-to-day use)**
- Full native run_turn / build_completion_request / tool dispatch loop that matches or exceeds the captured P4-0 harness shapes for todo_write, monitor, enter_plan_mode, spawn_agent (with persona + capability_mode), best-of-n, scheduler.
- Native scheduler / background task manager that feeds the existing lazy TerminalView + monitor ToolCall surface (no external PTY dependency for Zed-native sessions).
- Full skills loading/activation parity (already bridged; make native the primary for new work).
- Memory / persistent facts surface (GrokMemoryArtifacts + prompt injection) fully native and first-class.
- Session import/export/roundtrip with `~/.grok/sessions/` sqlite + jsonl formats (low-friction resume in both directions).

**P4-B. IDE Diagnostics as First-Class Agent Context (non-blocking, cross-language)**
- Extend the existing "Zed provides live LSP diagnostics" push (already done for Grok bridged/native) into a first-class, always-available context block for any native Grok profile thread.
- Surface rust-analyzer (and other language servers) errors/warnings directly in the system prompt / tool context on every turn, with risk classification.
- Replace or augment shell-based "run clippy" patterns with Zed-sourced lint data (faster, no blocking local builds, works for any language Zed supports).
- TDD + efficiency audit (O(1) on !grok paths).

**P4-C. Completion Notifications & Autonomy Discipline**
- System-level notifications (desktop + in-Zed) when a native Grok thread reaches a true "all current independent work is complete" state (the exact phrasing required by the three behavioral rules).
- Wire the existing `requires_explicit_completion_notification` + plan `has_pending_work` checks to trigger real OS notifications via GPUI.
- Strengthen the native enforcement path (matching the ACP kickback logic) so the model cannot silently stop while ZT-1 items remain.

**P4-D. Polish, Testing, Migration & Documentation**
- Full test coverage (unit + integration + E2E harness against captured artifacts) for every native Grok path (sub-agents, personas, monitors, plans, approvals, TurnId references, CWD classification, notifications).
- Migration tooling / "import from TUI session" flows that preserve full state.
- User-facing documentation + onboarding that clearly distinguishes "Grok (Bridged)" vs "Grok (Native)" and guides users to the superior native experience.
- Performance & latency validation (native must be noticeably lighter than ACP + external process).
- Remaining hygiene (any leftover internal TASK-*/tracking labels, full sync of living docs).

**Success Criteria for P4**
- A user can do 100% of their previous Grok Build TUI workflows entirely inside Zed using the native xAI Grok model profile, with superior visuals (ZT-1 surface, lazy monitors, persona badges, circular gauges, etc.), Turn + task traceability, correct CWD classification, and system notifications on true completion — without ever launching the external `grok` binary.
- All captured P4-0 fidelity requirements (tool shapes, persona/capability_mode, plan discipline, monitor semantics) are met or exceeded by the native implementation.
- IDE diagnostics are the preferred, non-blocking source of lint information for the agent.

**Immediate Next Steps (post this update)**
- Mark P3 bridging as fully closed in the backlog table.
- Launch Planning + Architecture sub-agent to break P4-A/B/C into the next 8–12 concrete, non-overlapping slices with clear file ownership.
- Continue the swarm model, always referencing TurnId/task-ids when coordinating across agents and turns.
